      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — trace-verdict-study\\n\\n> v2.7 — [UPDATED 2026-05-15] 整合 irreducible error 根因分析（GeneratorExit）和数据集重复率分析。数据集来源：HumanEval (164) + MBPP-sanitized (427) = 591 problems × 10 LLM-generated solutions = 5910 samples。CRUXEval 仅作为外部基准参照模型推理能力差异。\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nVerdict prediction (pass/fail) is fundamentally a **fault detection** task. The minimal sufficient signal is the **exception event** — a compact, structured diagnostic moment that achieves near-perfect accuracy (Qwen 99.83%, DeepSeek 99.83% at best checkpoint, cross-model gap = 0pp) regardless of model architecture. In contrast, richer trace representations that require deeper execution reasoning show model-dependent effectiveness: VOTA gap = 14.96pp, no_trace gap = 1.77pp. Information density — not training signal distribution — is the primary bottleneck: even oracle loss masking (label-only) cannot close the 13pp gap between full traces and targeted extraction. The design principle: **extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal; discard everything else.**\\n\\nVOTA's primary contribution is **fault indicator isolation** — automatically extracting exception events from noisy full traces — rather than generic trace compression. The \\\"compression helps\\\" narrative was a stepping stone; the real mechanism is diagnostic signal extraction.\\n\\n## Core Claims (3)\\n\\n### Claim 1: Exception-based fault detection is model-agnostic\\n\\nException summaries alone achieve near-perfect verdict prediction across architecturally distinct models: Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B (99.83%) and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B (99.83% at epoch1 best), cross-model gap = 0pp at best checkpoint, 0.50pp at final checkpoint. This contrasts with VOTA (gap = 14.96pp) and no_trace (gap = 1.77pp). Exception events are structurally simple (`[ERROR] type: ... message: ...`) and do not require deep trace reasoning — making them universally extractable.\\n\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: Qwen 99.83%, ECE=0.0027\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: DeepSeek 99.83% (epoch1 best) / 99.33% (epoch3 final)\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: DeepSeek VOTA 84.45% (gap = 14.96pp vs Qwen)\\n- `deepseek_no_trace_seed42`: DeepSeek no_trace 73.36% (gap = 1.77pp vs Qwen)\\n- `vota_no_ae_seed42`: 99.41% — actual/expected removal 不降，非 trivial string comparison\\n\\n**Irreducible error analysis** ✅: 3 seeds 错误的 2 个样本 (id=901, 905) 均来自 HumanEval/95 (`check_dict_case`)，根因为 Python `all()` 对 generator expression 短路触发 `GeneratorExit`（`BaseException` 子类，非真正错误）。tracer 将其标记为 `[ERROR]`，exception_only 模型看到 7 行 `[ERROR] GeneratorExit`，合理但错误地预测 fail。整个 1190 条测试集仅这 2 条含 `GeneratorExit`，模型无训练信号学习其良性本质。**这确认 99.83% 是 exception_only 表征的理论上限，剩余 0.17% 是 representation-inherent irreducible error，而非模型不足。**\\n\\n**Seed robustness** ✅: Qwen exception_only 3-seed 完全一致，构成本文最强 robustness 证据：\\n\\n| Seed | Accuracy | 95% CI | Errors |\\n|------|----------|--------|--------|\\n| 42 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\\n| 123 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\\n| 456 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\\n\\n3 seeds 错误样本 100% 重叠（id=901, 905, 均为 `check_dict_case` 函数, label=pass → pred=fail）。**Irreducible samples**: 模型已达数据集理论上限，variance = 0。\\n\\n**DeepSeek cross-model**: epoch1 best checkpoint 99.83% 与 Qwen 完全一致（gap = 0pp），进一步确认 exception signal 的 universality。epoch3 退化至 99.33%（gap = 0.50pp），见 D7 early stopping 讨论。\\n\\n### Claim 2: Information density, not gradient dilution, drives trace compression benefit\\n\\nFull traces help verdict prediction (81.76% [`full_trace_seed42_v5`], +6.6pp over baseline), but VOTA achieves 99.41% [`vota_seed42_v5`] (+24.3pp) — a +17.6pp advantage. Label-only loss ablation (plan_002) quantifies the causal decomposition: **gradient dilution contributes ~26% of the gap** (4.5pp; label-only full_trace = 86.22% [`full_trace_label_only_seed42`]), **information overload contributes ~74%** (13.0pp; only trace compression resolves this). Even with oracle loss masking that eliminates gradient dilution, full traces still underperform VOTA by 13pp.\\n\\nCross-model evidence reinforces this: VOTA gap (14.96pp) tracks base model execution reasoning capability (Qwen CRUXEval-I 56.5% vs DeepSeek 39.0%, Δ=17.5pp), suggesting trace utilization effectiveness is bounded by pretraining-derived inductive biases. Exception signal bypasses this bottleneck entirely (gap = 0pp at best checkpoint).\\n\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (gradient dilution fix, +4.5pp over standard full_trace)\\n- `full_trace_seed42_v5`: 81.76%, ECE=0.0958 (standard full_trace)\\n- `vota_seed42_v5`: 99.41%, ECE=0.0054\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (VOTA model-dependent)\\n- CRUXEval literature data (Wang et al.)\\n\\n### Claim 3: Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy for verdict prediction\\n\\nComponent ablation reveals verdict-relevant trace information has a strict two-tier hierarchy:\\n- **Tier 1 — diagnostic moment signals**: exception summaries (99.83% [`vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`]) and assertion-point information (99.92% [`vota_ae_only_seed42`]) are individually near-perfect and sufficient for verdict prediction. These signals are structurally simple and model-agnostic (exception cross-model gap = 0pp at best checkpoint).\\n- **Tier 2 — behavioral patterns**: loop compression (85.63% [`vota_loop_only_seed42`]) carries substantial signal but is model-dependent and fully subsumed by Tier 1.\\n\\nCombining tiers introduces noise rather than helping: exception_only (99.83%) > VOTA full (99.41%), p=0.01. Loop_only ≈ full_trace_label_only (85.63% vs 86.22%) confirms VOTA's loop compression precisely extracts the useful behavioral signal from full traces.\\n\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: 99.83%, ECE=0.0027 (Tier 1 exception)\\n- `vota_ae_only_seed42`: 99.92%, ECE=0.0004 (Tier 1 assertion-point, 与 exception 统计等价 p=0.80)\\n- `vota_loop_only_seed42`: 85.63%, ECE=0.0831 (Tier 2 behavioral)\\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (≈ loop_only, confirming extraction equivalence)\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: 99.83% epoch1 / 99.33% epoch3 (Tier 1 cross-model validation)\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (Tier 1+2 cross-model: Tier 2 introduces model-dependent degradation)\\n\\n**Calibration**: 完整 calibration 数据确认 Tier 1 信号不仅 accuracy 近完美，置信度校准也近完美：\\n\\n| Condition | Accuracy | ECE | Brier | 解读 |\\n|-----------|----------|-----|-------|------|\\n| ae_only | 99.92% | 0.0004 | 0.0008 | 近乎完美校准 |\\n| exception_only | 99.83% | 0.0027 | 0.0013 | 近乎完美校准 |\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 0.0054 | 0.005 | 优秀，但 Tier 2 引入轻微 noise |\\n| loop_only | 85.63% | 0.0831 | — | Tier 2 单独校准中等 |\\n| label_only | 86.22% | 0.1107 | — | gradient dilution fix 仍 miscalibrated |\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | 0.0958 | — | 中度 miscalibrated |\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 0.1715 | 0.1972 | 严重 miscalibrated |\\n\\nno_trace ECE 是 exception_only 的 **~63x**（0.1715/0.0027），Brier 是 **~150x**（0.1972/0.0013）。Trace signal（尤其 Tier 1）不仅提升 accuracy，更从根本上改善模型 calibration。\\n\\n**Design principle**: verdict-optimized traces should extract highest-specificity diagnostic signals; lower-specificity signals are redundant and may introduce noise.\\n\\n## Cross-Model Evidence Summary\\n\\n| Condition | Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B | DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B | Cross-model gap | Interpretation |\\n|-----------|-------------------|---------------------|-----------------|----------------|\\n| exception_only | 99.83% | 99.83% (ep1) / 99.33% (ep3) | 0pp (best) / 0.50pp (final) | **Universal** — model-agnostic |\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 84.45% | -14.96pp | Model-dependent — requires execution reasoning |\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 73.36% | -1.77pp | Baseline gap tracks general capability |\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | collapsed (v3 pending) | N/A | DeepSeek v1 label truncation bug |\\n\\n**Key insight**: cross-model gap 与 signal complexity 正相关。Structurally simple signals (exception) transfer perfectly; signals requiring execution reasoning (VOTA loop compression) degrade proportional to CRUXEval reasoning gap (17.5pp).\\n\\n## VOTA Repositioning\\n\\n**旧叙事**: VOTA 是 trace compression 方法，价值在于\\\"压缩使 trace 更有效\\\"。\\n\\n**新叙事**: VOTA 是 **fault indicator isolation** 方法，核心价值在于自动从 noisy execution traces 中提取 exception events 等 diagnostic signals。\\n\\n**证据链**:\\n1. exception_only (99.83%) ≥ VOTA full (99.41%) — 压缩后的其他组件（loop, ae）不仅无益，反而引入 noise\\n2. VOTA 跨模型 gap = 14.96pp — 因为 VOTA 包含 model-dependent 的 Tier 2 组件（loop compression 需要 execution reasoning）\\n3. exception_only 跨模型 gap = 0pp — isolate 出 Tier 1 后，model capability 不再是瓶颈\\n4. vota_no_ae (99.41%) = VOTA (99.41%) — actual/expected 移除无影响，exception 是真正的 active ingredient\\n\\n**论文中的表述**: \\\"VOTA's trace compression pipeline incidentally isolates exception events — the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal. Its value lies not in making traces shorter, but in separating high-specificity fault indicators from low-specificity behavioral patterns.\\\"\\n\\n**对 Method section 的影响**: VOTA 仍然完整描述（loop compression + exception + actual/expected），但 Results/Discussion 明确指出 exception isolation 是核心机制，其他组件是 model-capability-dependent bonus。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Existing trace SFT studies (DCSH, TracePile) evaluate only generative tasks and find limited, model-dependent benefit. No prior work has tested trace SFT on verdict tasks (binary pass/fail), nor identified what trace components drive classification performance.\\n\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: DCSH (EMNLP Findings 2025) tested 5 trace formats on generation tasks, finding that \\\"no single trace representation consistently outperforms others.\\\" TracePile (NeurIPS 2025) scaled to 2.6M samples but only evaluated generation. TRACED (ICSE 2024) showed traces help encoder-based classification. All existing trace formats are designed for execution simulation, not fault detection.\\n\\n- **Our framing**: We reframe verdict prediction as **fault detection** and ask: what is the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal? Through systematic component ablation across two architecturally distinct models, we discover that exception events — compact, structured error summaries — are both necessary and sufficient, achieving near-perfect accuracy (99.83% on both models at best checkpoint) with zero cross-model gap. Richer trace representations work only when the base model has sufficient execution reasoning capability, making them unreliable for general deployment.\\n\\n- **Headline result**: \\\"Exception events achieve 99.83% verdict accuracy on both Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B (cross-model gap = 0pp), while VOTA traces show a 14.96pp gap between the same models — demonstrating that fault indicator isolation, not trace reasoning, is the robust mechanism for verdict prediction.\\\"\\n\\n## Story Arc\\n\\n1. **Setup**: Trace SFT for code tasks — promising but inconsistent in prior work (DCSH, TracePile). Unknown whether traces help verdict tasks and what trace design matters.\\n\\n2. **First result — traces help, compression helps more** (single model): no_trace 75.13% → full_trace 81.76% → VOTA 99.41%. Label-only loss ablation decomposes the gap: 26% gradient dilution, 74% information overload. Compression improves accuracy through higher information density, not just cost savings.\\n\\n3. **Twist — what component actually drives VOTA?** Component ablation reveals exception_only (99.83%) ≥ full VOTA (99.41%). The \\\"trace reasoning\\\" narrative is wrong — verdict prediction is fault detection. Tier 1 (exceptions + assertion-point) subsumes Tier 2 (loops). Actual/expected removal doesn't hurt (99.41%).\\n\\n4. **Cross-model stress test — the universal vs the contingent**: Exception_only transfers with zero gap (0pp at best checkpoint), but VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek. The model-agnostic component is precisely the one that doesn't require trace reasoning. CRUXEval reasoning gap (17.5pp) parallels VOTA gap — trace utilization is bounded by pretraining. Seed robustness (3-seed variance = 0) + calibration (ECE=0.0027) 双重确认 exception signal 的 reliability。\\n\\n5. **Design principle**: For verdict tasks, identify the highest-specificity diagnostic signal (exception events) and discard everything else. VOTA's value is fault indicator isolation — automatically extracting exceptions from noisy traces — not generic compression. Simpler signals are more robust; complexity tolerance depends on model capability.\\n\\n## Dataset Characteristics\\n\\n### Data duplication analysis\\n\\n| Condition | Total | Unique | Duplication rate | Cause |\\n|-----------|-------|--------|------------------|-------|\\n| exception_only_train | 4720 | 2858 | 39.45% | 同题多个 passing 解映射为相同 (input, output) — 无异常 → 同一 trace |\\n| exception_only_test | 1190 | 607 | 48.99% | 同上 |\\n| raw_traces | 5910 | 5803 | 1.81% | 完整 trace 含行号/变量值/内存地址，几乎不重复 |\\n\\n**Train-test input overlap: 0（无数据泄漏）**\\n\\n**解读**: exception_only 高重复率是 representation 的自然属性，非数据问题。多个正确解对同一测试用例执行时若均无异常，exception_only 表征退化为相同的空/无异常标记。这恰好说明 exception signal 的 compactness — 它仅编码 fault presence，不编码 solution identity。raw_traces 重复率极低 (1.81%) 因为完整 trace 包含 solution-specific 信息（变量值、内存地址）。尽管高重复率意味着有效训练样本少于名义数量，模型仍达 99.83% — 进一步确认 exception signal 的 learnability。\\n\\n## DeepSeek full_trace 处理\\n\\n**现状**: v1 坍塌（28.5%, 100% 预测 fail, 根因: 训练端 label 截断）。v2 重训启动但被 kill（Loop #6 ops worker 腾 GPU 给 vota_seed456）。\\n\\n**处理方案**: 下一个 GPU 空闲（deepseek_exception_only_seed123 完成后 ~20:15 UTC）立即重启 deepseek_full_trace_v3。\\n\\n**对论文叙事的影响**: 无. 三个有效条件 (exception_only, VOTA, no_trace) 已足够支撑全部 claims. DeepSeek full_trace 结果是 bonus, 非必需. 若 v3 在投稿前完成, 纳入跨模型对比表 (4/4 conditions). 否则报告为 N/A 并在 Limitations 中透明披露根因.\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n\\n| Risk | Assessment | Mitigation |\\n|------|-----------|------------|\\n| 单任务 | 仍然只在 test verdict prediction 上验证 | 跨模型验证部分弥补; Discussion 中讨论 generalization |\\n| 单数据集 | HumanEval + MBPP-sanitized 是唯一数据源 (591 problems × 10 solutions) | 定位为 empirical study, 深度优于广度; 两个标准 benchmark 组合覆盖多样编程题型; exception_only 有效训练样本仅 2858 unique 即达 99.83%, 暗示 signal 的 simplicity 降低对数据量的依赖 |\\n| \\\"只是 feature engineering\\\" | exception extraction 不复杂 | simplicity 本身是 contribution — 最简信号最 robust |\\n| Negative result 味道 | VOTA 跨模型不 robust | Reframe: 识别 robust vs non-robust 组件边界是 positive |\\n| Seed robustness | ✅ 已解决: 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds), irreducible samples 确认 | — |\\n| Calibration | ✅ 完整数据: Tier 1 ECE≤0.003, no_trace ECE=0.171 (~63x gap) | Brier ~150x gap 进一步确认 |\\n| 只用 2 个模型 | 有限但有意义的 diversity | Architecture + pretraining data (5.5T vs 2T) + CRUXEval gap (17.5pp) 构成充分差异 |\\n| 数据重复率 | exception_only 训练集 39.45% 重复 | 非数据泄漏（train-test overlap = 0）；是 representation compactness 的自然结果；有效样本仍足够学习 |\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n| Concern | Planned Response |\\n|---------|-----------------|\\n| \\\"exception_only 99.83% 是不是 trivial pattern matching [ERROR] 有无？\\\" | full_trace 也包含 [ERROR] 但仅 81.76%——模型需要从 trace context 中 extract 和 utilize exception signal, 不是简单 keyword detection. no_trace 无 exception, 75.13%. |\\n| \\\"为什么 exception_only > VOTA? adding info 反而降?\\\" | 非 exception 组件 (loop/ae tokens) 稀释 attention 对 exception 信号的聚焦. Calibration: exception_only ECE=0.0027 vs VOTA ECE=0.0054. 部分样本 loop 正常但有 exception, 引入矛盾信号. |\\n| \\\"CRUXEval gap 与 VOTA gap 的相关性可能是 coincidence\\\" | 明确标注为 hypothesis. 列出 alternatives (tokenizer efficiency, pretraining scale 2.75x, chat template). 不 overclaim. |\\n| \\\"只有 2 个模型，universality claim 太强\\\" | 同意是 limitation. 两模型在 architecture (RoPE vs ALiBi), pretraining data (5.5T vs 2T), execution reasoning (CRUXEval 17.5pp gap) 上有充分差异, 不是 trivial replication. Exception signal 的 structural simplicity 提供 inductive argument for broader generalization. |\\n| \\\"99.83% 可能 saturated, 看不出 exception 到底学了什么\\\" | Calibration 双指标: exception_only ECE=0.0027, Brier=0.0013 (近完美校准). 对比 no_trace ECE=0.1715, Brier=0.1972 (~63x/~150x gap). **Irreducible error 根因已明确**: 2/1190 errors 均因 Python `all()` 短路触发 `GeneratorExit`（`BaseException` 子类，非真正错误），tracer 将其标记为 `[ERROR]`。模型看到 7 行 `[ERROR] GeneratorExit` 合理预测 fail，但代码实际正确。整个测试集仅这 2 条含 `GeneratorExit`，无训练信号可学习其良性本质。99.83% 是 representation 的理论上限，非 saturation artifact。 |\\n| \\\"只用了 1 个 seed\\\" | ✅ 已解决: 3 seeds 结果完全一致 (99.83% ± 0.00), irreducible samples 100% 重叠, 95% CI 均为 [99.58, 100]. |\\n| \\\"eval_loss 上升但 accuracy 提升——checkpoint selection 有问题?\\\" | Discussion D1: token-level loss 与 binary verdict accuracy 解耦. 推荐 verdict-specific metric. Methodological contribution. |\\n| \\\"DeepSeek full_trace 坍塌是 bug 不是 finding\\\" | 诚实披露根因 (训练端 label 截断). 其他 3 条件不受影响. 修复版本如就绪则补入. |\\n| \\\"calibration 数据只有 1 个 seed\\\" | Seed robustness 已证明 accuracy variance=0 (3 seeds), calibration 在 accuracy 完全一致的前提下 expected 也一致. ECE/Brier 的极小值 (0.0027/0.0013) 留有极小 variance 空间. |\\n| \\\"DeepSeek epoch1 > epoch3, 为什么用 epoch3 报告?\\\" | 正文用 final checkpoint (consistent criterion). Discussion D7 专门讨论 early stopping: epoch1=99.83% 说明 exception signal 1 epoch 即可充分学习, 延长训练反而引入 mild overfitting. 补充 best-checkpoint 对比 (gap=0pp). |\\n| \\\"exception_only 数据集 ~40-49% 重复，有效训练样本不足？\\\" | 重复源自 representation compactness：同题多个 passing 解均无异常 → 相同 trace。**非数据泄漏**（train-test input overlap = 0）。raw_traces 仅 1.81% 重复，确认重复是 exception_only 表征特性而非数据收集问题。模型在有效样本仅 2858 unique 的条件下仍达 99.83%，反而说明 exception signal 的高 learnability — 少量 unique 样本即可充分学习 fault detection pattern。 |\\n\\n## Discussion 要点\\n\\n### D1: eval_loss vs verdict accuracy 解耦\\nDeepSeek VOTA: eval_loss epoch1→3 从 0.068 升至 0.112 (+65%), 但 accuracy 从 78.15% 升至 84.45% (+6.3pp). Token-level calibration 与 binary classification accuracy 方向相反. **Implication**: verdict tasks 应用 task-specific metric (accuracy/F1) 做 checkpoint selection, 非 eval_loss.\\n\\n### D2: CRUXEval gap 假说\\nVOTA 跨模型 gap (14.96pp) 与 CRUXEval execution reasoning gap (15-17.5pp) 数值吻合. 假说: trace utilization effectiveness 受 base model \\\"execution reasoning readiness\\\" 约束. **标注**: hypothesis, N=2, 列出 alternatives. 不 overclaim. **注意**: 我们的训练/测试数据来自 HumanEval + MBPP-sanitized（非 CRUXEval），CRUXEval-I 分数仅作为衡量两个模型 execution reasoning 能力差异的外部参照。论文中必须明确区分数据来源与外部基准引用。\\n\\n### D3: Simplicity as robustness\\nException signal 跨模型 robust 恰因其 structurally simple. 暗示 **trace design 应匹配 deployment model 的能力下界**, 而非上界.\\n\\n### D4: Checkpoint selection 与 gap 报告\\n正文 exception_only 跨模型 gap 用 final-to-final = 0.50pp (一致 criterion). 补充: at best checkpoint (DeepSeek epoch1), gap = 0pp. DeepSeek epoch1→3 退化 0.50pp 与 D1 eval_loss 解耦现象一致.\\n\\n### D5: Irreducible samples — GeneratorExit 根因分析\\n3 seeds 的 2/1190 errors 完全相同（id=901, 905, 均来自 HumanEval/95 `check_dict_case`），方向一致（pass→fail false negatives）。\\n\\n**根因**: Python `all()` 对 generator expression 短路时丢弃未耗尽的 generator，触发 `GeneratorExit`。`GeneratorExit` 继承自 `BaseException`（非 `Exception`），是 Python 内部控制流机制而非真正错误。但 tracer 对所有 caught exceptions 统一标记 `[ERROR]`，导致 exception_only 模型看到 7 行 `[ERROR] GeneratorExit`，合理但错误地预测 fail。\\n\\n**为何不可约**:\\n1. **表征局限**: exception_only 条件下模型仅见异常数据，无法访问控制流、返回值或 assertion 结果。`GeneratorExit` 在此表征中与真正错误不可区分。\\n2. **跨 seed 确定性**: 3 个 random seeds 产生完全相同的 errors，确认这是 data/representation 属性而非模型初始化随机性。\\n3. **训练集无信号**: 整个 1190 条测试集仅这 2 条含 `GeneratorExit`，训练集中也缺乏足够的 `GeneratorExit` 良性样本供模型学习。\\n4. **人类也会犯错**: 仅看 exception trace（7 行 `[ERROR]`），人类审查者在不了解 Python generator protocol 的情况下也会预测 fail。\\n\\n**对论文的意义**: (1) 99.83% 是 exception_only representation 在此数据集上的理论 ceiling，非 saturation artifact；(2) 揭示 tracer 设计的改进方向——区分 `BaseException` 子类（如 `GeneratorExit`, `KeyboardInterrupt`）与 `Exception` 子类（真正的程序错误）可消除此类 irreducible error；(3) 提供 concrete evidence 说明 exception_only 模型\\\"理解\\\"的是 `[ERROR]` tag 的 fault indicator 语义，而非 exception type 的 fine-grained 含义。\\n\\n### D6: Calibration as secondary evidence\\nCalibration 数据提供独立于 accuracy 的 evidence dimension。no_trace Brier=0.1972 说明模型无 trace 时不仅预测错，且对错误预测过度自信。exception_only Brier=0.0013 说明模型在有 exception signal 时几乎完美区分确定性和不确定性。这支持 Claim 1 的 \\\"sufficient signal\\\" 论点——exception 不仅让模型预测对，还让模型 \\\"知道自己对\\\"。\\n\\n### D7: Early stopping for exception signal\\nDeepSeek exception_only: epoch1 = 99.83%, epoch3 = 99.33%（退化 0.50pp）。Exception signal 结构简单，1 epoch 即充分学习；延长训练导致 token-level overfitting 反而轻微损害 binary classification。与 D1 的 eval_loss 解耦现象一致。**Implication**: 对 structurally simple diagnostic signals，early stopping 不仅节省计算，可能还提升性能。论文建议: verdict SFT 应 monitor task-specific metric (accuracy) 而非 training loss，尤其当训练信号高度 structured 时。对比: VOTA 在 DeepSeek 上 epoch1→3 accuracy 持续上升 (78.15%→84.45%)，说明 complex signals 确实需要更多 epochs——early stopping 的最优点取决于 signal complexity。\\n\\n### D8: Data duplication as representation signature [NEW]\\nexception_only 训练集 39.45% 重复（4720→2858 unique），测试集 48.99%（1190→607 unique），而 raw_traces 仅 1.81%（5910→5803 unique）。重复源自 exception_only 表征的 design-by-intent compactness：同一编程题的多个 passing 解执行时均无异常，exception_only 表征退化为相同的空标记。这一数据特征本身就是 Claim 3 two-tier hierarchy 的佐证——Tier 1 信号仅编码 fault presence/absence，不编码 solution-level details，因此天然高重复。Train-test input overlap = 0 排除数据泄漏。\\n\\n## Contingency Framing (已解决, 留存档)\\n\\n### Claim 1 — RESOLVED (D008 → D010 → D019)\\nvota_no_ae 99.41% 否定 trivial comparison. exception_only 99.83% 揭示核心机制. 跨模型 0pp gap (best checkpoint) 确认 universality.\\n\\n### Claim 2 — RESOLVED (D006 + D009)\\nv5 full_trace 81.76%. plan_002 label-only 86.22%, gradient dilution = minor (26%). 跨模型 VOTA gap 补充证据.\\n\\n## 与 v1 Framing 的关键差异\\n\\n| 维度 | v1 | v2.7 |\\n|------|----|----|\\n| Headline | VOTA compression 优于 full trace | Exception events are universal diagnostic signals |\\n| Primary claim | VOTA +24pp through fault indicator preservation | Exception signal transfers across models (gap = 0pp) |\\n| Claim 2 | Compression > full trace (只有 gradient dilution) | Information density primary + gradient dilution quantification + cross-model reinforcement |\\n| Claim 3 Tier 1 | exception_only only | exception + Continuing through the remaining results: the full_trace, no_trace, and ablation variants (ae_only, loop_only, label_only) all match their registry values, and the DeepSeek model results across different configurations also check out against the recorded data.

Looking at the actual deduplication rates from the data: the exception_only_train set has a 39.45% duplicate rate and the test set has 48.99% duplicates, with no train-test input overlap. That high duplicate rate in the test set is significant because it means the effective sample size is smaller than the reported N=1190, which could matter for statistical significance interpretation. Though the paper does use Wilson confidence intervals and bootstrap tests, which should remain valid despite the duplicates.

The key insight is that these duplicates aren't random—they arise because passing solutions for the same problem generate identical exception traces (no exceptions), while failing solutions each produce unique exceptions. So the duplicates are mostly "pass" samples that the model correctly predicts, while the 2 errors (IDs 901 and 905) represent unique inputs. This means the reported error rate isn't misleading in the way I initially thought, since the model's predictions on duplicate inputs would be identical anyway.

However, there's still a statistical concern: with only 607 unique inputs instead of 1190, the effective sample size for measuring accuracy is smaller, which should widen the confidence intervals. The identical accuracy across seeds might also be partially explained by the model seeing the same inputs repeatedly. And comparing this condition to others with different duplication rates could introduce unfairness. I should check whether the paper addresses deduplication and look at the method section for how the dataset is described.

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We presented the first systematic study of what execution trace components drive verdict prediction performance.
4	Through component ablation across two code LLMs, we established three findings:
5	
6	\textbf{(1) Exception signal universality.}
7	Exception events alone achieve 99.83\% (Qwen) and 99.33\% (DeepSeek) accuracy with a cross-model gap of only 0.50pp, demonstrating that this signal transfers across model families.
8	In contrast, the full VOTA representation degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model, revealing that mixed trace processing is model-dependent.
9	
10	\textbf{(2) Information density decomposition.}
11	The 17.65pp gap between full traces and VOTA decomposes into gradient dilution (26\%, from training on irrelevant trace tokens) and information overload (74\%, from processing irrelevant trace lines at inference).
12	The primary bottleneck is trace content selection, not loss design.
13	
14	\textbf{(3) Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy.}
15	Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\geq}$99.8\% accuracy, while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach 85.6\%.
16	Combining tiers in VOTA slightly degrades performance (99.41\%), confirming that additional trace components introduce noise rather than complementary signal.
17	
18	These results yield a practical design principle: for verdict prediction, extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal and discard everything else.
19	Future work should test whether exception dominance extends to other datasets (DebugBench, LiveCodeBench), model scales, and classification tasks beyond verdict prediction.
```

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	
3	\subsection{Task Definition}
4	
5	We study \emph{verdict prediction}: given a Python function, a test input, and an optional execution trace, predict whether the test passes or fails.
6	The model produces a single token---\texttt{pass} or \texttt{fail}---making this a binary classification task.
7	Unlike code generation \citep{chen2025tracepile} or output prediction \citep{gu2024cruxeval}, verdict prediction isolates the model's ability to detect faults from its ability to simulate execution, providing a cleaner lens on what trace information actually contributes.
8	
9	\subsection{Dataset}
10	\label{sec:dataset}
11	
12	We sampled 10 candidate solutions per problem from Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct (temperature 0.8, top\_p 0.95) for 591 problems from HumanEval \citep{chen2021evaluating} (164 problems) and MBPP-sanitized \citep{austin2021program} (427 problems), executed each solution against canonical test cases with \texttt{sys.settrace} to obtain line-level execution traces and pass/fail verdicts, yielding 5,910 trace--verdict pairs (67\% pass, 33\% fail).
13	The dataset is split 80/20 by problem ID into 4,720 training and 1,190 test samples; the test set majority-class baseline is 71.51\%.
14	
15	\paragraph{Deduplication statistics.}
16	Because multiple passing solutions to the same problem produce identical (input, output) pairs in the \texttt{exception\_only} format---correct executions raise no exceptions, so the trace content is the same regardless of the solution's internal logic---the dataset contains duplicate rows.
17	Table~\ref{tab:dedup} reports the duplication rates across formats.
18	Train--test input overlap is zero (split by problem ID), so no information leakage occurs.
19	The high duplication in \texttt{exception\_only} does not affect our experimental conclusions: after deduplication the training set still contains 2,858 unique samples and the test set 607, providing sufficient coverage for the 591-problem benchmark.
20	We report these statistics for transparency.
21	
22	\begin{table}[t]
23	\centering
24	\small
25	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
26	\toprule
27	\textbf{Split} & \textbf{Total} & \textbf{Unique} & \textbf{Dup.\ rate} \\
28	\midrule
29	exception\_only train & 4{,}720 & 2{,}858 & 39.45\% \\
30	exception\_only test  & 1{,}190 &   607 & 48.99\% \\
31	raw\_traces (full)    & 5{,}910 & 5{,}803 &  1.81\% \\
32	\bottomrule
33	\end{tabular}
34	\caption{Deduplication statistics. The \texttt{exception\_only} format collapses multiple passing solutions into identical rows because correct executions produce no exception events. Train--test input overlap is 0 (no leakage).}
35	\label{tab:dedup}
36	\end{table}
37	
38	\subsection{Trace Representations}
39	
40	We define seven input conditions that systematically vary the trace information available to the model (Figure~\ref{fig:trace_overview}):
41	
42	\begin{itemize}
43	\item \texttt{no\_trace}: Source code and test input only; no execution information.
44	\item \texttt{full\_trace}: Complete line-by-line execution trace recording variable states at each step.
45	\item \texttt{VOTA} (Verdict-Oriented Trace Abstraction): Three compressed components---exception events, assertion-point comparisons, and loop compression summaries.
46	\item \texttt{exception\_only}: Only exception and error events extracted from the trace (e.g., \texttt{[ERROR] TypeError: ...}).
47	\item \texttt{ae\_only}: Only assertion-point actual/expected value pairs from the final comparison.
48	\item \texttt{loop\_only}: Only loop compression summaries (iteration counts and final variable states).
49	\item \texttt{VOTA\_no\_ae}: VOTA without assertion-point information (exception events + loop compression).
50	\end{itemize}
51	
52	These conditions span a spectrum from no trace information (\texttt{no\_trace}) through maximally compressed signals (\texttt{exception\_only}) to the complete unfiltered trace (\texttt{full\_trace}).
53	
54	\begin{figure*}[t]
55	\centering
56	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig1_trace_overview}
57	\caption{Overview of the verdict prediction pipeline. Source code and a test input are executed to produce a full raw trace. Four experimental conditions vary the trace information provided to the model: \texttt{no\_trace} (code only, 75.13\%), \texttt{full\_trace} (complete trace, 81.76\%), VOTA (compressed components, 99.41\%), and \texttt{exception\_only} (exception events only, 99.83\%). Each condition is paired with the same model to predict a pass/fail verdict. Accuracies shown are for Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B (seed=42, $N{=}1{,}190$).}
58	\label{fig:trace_overview}
59	\end{figure*}
60	
61	\subsection{Models}
62	
63	We evaluate two instruction-tuned code LLMs at the 7B parameter scale:
64	
65	\begin{itemize}
66	\item \textbf{Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct} \citep{qwen2025coder}: Pretrained on 5.5T tokens. Serves as the primary model for all ablation experiments.
67	\item \textbf{DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct} \citep{deepseek2024coder}: Pretrained on 2T tokens. Used for cross-model validation to test whether findings generalize beyond a single model family.
68	\end{itemize}
69	
70	The two models differ primarily in pretraining data volume (5.5T vs.\ 2T tokens), providing a natural axis for evaluating whether trace signal utility is model-dependent \citep{dong2024composition}.
71	
72	\subsection{Training Setup}
73	
74	All conditions use identical training configurations to ensure fair comparison.
75	We apply LoRA \citep{hu2022lora} (rank=16, $\alpha$=32, dropout=0.05) to all linear layers and train for 3 epochs with batch size 4, gradient accumulation 8 (effective batch size 32), learning rate $2 \times 10^{-4}$ with linear warmup and linear decay, warmup ratio 0.1, and weight decay 0.01.
76	The maximum sequence length is 2,048 tokens; input sequences exceeding this limit are truncated from the input content while preserving the complete verdict label.
77	Training wall-clock time ranges from 0.5 to 2.5 hours per condition on a single GPU, depending on trace format verbosity.
78	The primary seed is 42; seed robustness is evaluated with seeds 123 and 456 for the \texttt{exception\_only} condition.
79	
80	\subsection{Loss Ablation}
81	
82	To disentangle training signal quality from input signal quality, we introduce a \texttt{full\_trace\_label\_only} variant.
83	This condition uses the same full trace as input but computes loss only on the verdict token, masking all trace tokens during training (loss weight = 0 on trace positions).
84	If this variant improves over standard \texttt{full\_trace}, the improvement is attributable to \emph{gradient dilution}---trace tokens diluting the training signal without contributing to verdict prediction.
85	The remaining gap to VOTA is then attributable to \emph{information overload}---irrelevant trace content that the model must process at inference time.
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We presented the first systematic study of what execution trace components drive verdict prediction performance.
4	Through component ablation across two code LLMs, we established three findings:
5	
6	\textbf{(1) Exception signal universality.}
7	Exception events alone achieve 99.83\% (Qwen) and 99.33\% (DeepSeek) accuracy with a cross-model gap of only 0.50pp, demonstrating that this signal transfers across model families.
8	In contrast, the full VOTA representation degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model, revealing that mixed trace processing is model-dependent.
9	
10	\textbf{(2) Information density decomposition.}
11	The 17.65pp gap between full traces and VOTA decomposes into gradient dilution (26\%, from training on irrelevant trace tokens) and information overload (74\%, from processing irrelevant trace lines at inference).
12	The primary bottleneck is trace content selection, not loss design.
13	
14	\textbf{(3) Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy.}
15	Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\geq}$99.8\% accuracy, while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach 85.6\%.
16	Combining tiers in VOTA slightly degrades performance (99.41\%), confirming that additional trace components introduce noise rather than complementary signal.
17	
18	These results yield a practical design principle: for verdict prediction, extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal and discard everything else.
19	Future work should test whether exception dominance extends to other datasets (DebugBench, LiveCodeBench), model scales, and classification tasks beyond verdict prediction.
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	
3	\subsection{Task Definition}
4	
5	We study \emph{verdict prediction}: given a Python function, a test input, and an optional execution trace, predict whether the test passes or fails.
6	The model produces a single token---\texttt{pass} or \texttt{fail}---making this a binary classification task.
7	Unlike code generation \citep{chen2025tracepile} or output prediction \citep{gu2024cruxeval}, verdict prediction isolates the model's ability to detect faults from its ability to simulate execution, providing a cleaner lens on what trace information actually contributes.
8	
9	\subsection{Dataset}
10	\label{sec:dataset}
11	
12	We sampled 10 candidate solutions per problem from Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct (temperature 0.8, top\_p 0.95) for 591 problems from HumanEval \citep{chen2021evaluating} (164 problems) and MBPP-sanitized \citep{austin2021program} (427 problems), executed each solution against canonical test cases with \texttt{sys.settrace} to obtain line-level execution traces and pass/fail verdicts, yielding 5,910 trace--verdict pairs (67\% pass, 33\% fail).
13	The dataset is split 80/20 by problem ID into 4,720 training and 1,190 test samples; the test set majority-class baseline is 71.51\%.
14	
15	\paragraph{Deduplication statistics.}
16	Because multiple passing solutions to the same problem produce identical (input, output) pairs in the \texttt{exception\_only} format---correct executions raise no exceptions, so the trace content is the same regardless of the solution's internal logic---the dataset contains duplicate rows.
17	Table~\ref{tab:dedup} reports the duplication rates across formats.
18	Train--test input overlap is zero (split by problem ID), so no information leakage occurs.
19	The high duplication in \texttt{exception\_only} does not affect our experimental conclusions: after deduplication the training set still contains 2,858 unique samples and the test set 607, providing sufficient coverage for the 591-problem benchmark.
20	We report these statistics for transparency.
21	
22	\begin{table}[t]
23	\centering
24	\small
25	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
26	\toprule
27	\textbf{Split} & \textbf{Total} & \textbf{Unique} & \textbf{Dup.\ rate} \\
28	\midrule
29	exception\_only train & 4{,}720 & 2{,}858 & 39.45\% \\
30	exception\_only test  & 1{,}190 &   607 & 48.99\% \\
31	raw\_traces (full)    & 5{,}910 & 5{,}803 &  1.81\% \\
32	\bottomrule
33	\end{tabular}
34	\caption{Deduplication statistics. The \texttt{exception\_only} format collapses multiple passing solutions into identical rows because correct executions produce no exception events. Train--test input overlap is 0 (no leakage).}
35	\label{tab:dedup}
36	\end{table}
37	
38	\subsection{Trace Representations}
39	
40	We define seven input conditions that systematically vary the trace information available to the model (Figure~\ref{fig:trace_overview}):
41	
42	\begin{itemize}
43	\item \texttt{no\_trace}: Source code and test input only; no execution information.
44	\item \texttt{full\_trace}: Complete line-by-line execution trace recording variable states at each step.
45	\item \texttt{VOTA} (Verdict-Oriented Trace Abstraction): Three compressed components---exception events, assertion-point comparisons, and loop compression summaries.
46	\item \texttt{exception\_only}: Only exception and error events extracted from the trace (e.g., \texttt{[ERROR] TypeError: ...}).
47	\item \texttt{ae\_only}: Only assertion-point actual/expected value pairs from the final comparison.
48	\item \texttt{loop\_only}: Only loop compression summaries (iteration counts and final variable states).
49	\item \texttt{VOTA\_no\_ae}: VOTA without assertion-point information (exception events + loop compression).
50	\end{itemize}
51	
52	These conditions span a spectrum from no trace information (\texttt{no\_trace}) through maximally compressed signals (\texttt{exception\_only}) to the complete unfiltered trace (\texttt{full\_trace}).
53	
54	\begin{figure*}[t]
55	\centering
56	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig1_trace_overview}
57	\caption{Overview of the verdict prediction pipeline. Source code and a test input are executed to produce a full raw trace. Four experimental conditions vary the trace information provided to the model: \texttt{no\_trace} (code only, 75.13\%), \texttt{full\_trace} (complete trace, 81.76\%), VOTA (compressed components, 99.41\%), and \texttt{exception\_only} (exception events only, 99.83\%). Each condition is paired with the same model to predict a pass/fail verdict. Accuracies shown are for Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B (seed=42, $N{=}1{,}190$).}
58	\label{fig:trace_overview}
59	\end{figure*}
60	
61	\subsection{Models}
62	
63	We evaluate two instruction-tuned code LLMs at the 7B parameter scale:
64	
65	\begin{itemize}
66	\item \textbf{Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct} \citep{qwen2025coder}: Pretrained on 5.5T tokens. Serves as the primary model for all ablation experiments.
67	\item \textbf{DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct} \citep{deepseek2024coder}: Pretrained on 2T tokens. Used for cross-model validation to test whether findings generalize beyond a single model family.
68	\end{itemize}
69	
70	The two models differ primarily in pretraining data volume (5.5T vs.\ 2T tokens), providing a natural axis for evaluating whether trace signal utility is model-dependent \citep{dong2024composition}.
71	
72	\subsection{Training Setup}
73	
74	All conditions use identical training configurations to ensure fair comparison.
75	We apply LoRA \citep{hu2022lora} (rank=16, $\alpha$=32, dropout=0.05) to all linear layers and train for 3 epochs with batch size 4, gradient accumulation 8 (effective batch size 32), learning rate $2 \times 10^{-4}$ with linear warmup and linear decay, warmup ratio 0.1, and weight decay 0.01.
76	The maximum sequence length is 2,048 tokens; input sequences exceeding this limit are truncated from the input content while preserving the complete verdict label.
77	Training wall-clock time ranges from 0.5 to 2.5 hours per condition on a single GPU, depending on trace format verbosity.
78	The primary seed is 42; seed robustness is evaluated with seeds 123 and 456 for the \texttt{exception\_only} condition.
79	
80	\subsection{Loss Ablation}
81	
82	To disentangle training signal quality from input signal quality, we introduce a \texttt{full\_trace\_label\_only} variant.
83	This condition uses the same full trace as input but computes loss only on the verdict token, masking all trace tokens during training (loss weight = 0 on trace positions).
84	If this variant improves over standard \texttt{full\_trace}, the improvement is attributable to \emph{gradient dilution}---trace tokens diluting the training signal without contributing to verdict prediction.
85	The remaining gap to VOTA is then attributable to \emph{information overload}---irrelevant trace content that the model must process at inference time.
```

</details>

Now I have a complete picture of the paper. Let me assess the worker's patch:

**Verification of key claims against registry:**

All numbers check out:
- exception_only accuracies match across seeds ✅
- VOTA, full_trace, no_trace numbers match ✅


- DeepSeek numbers match ✅
- Calibration numbers match (properly rounded) ✅

**Issues to flag:**

The dedup statistics are transparently presented in the methods section with a clear explanation of why exception_only produces high duplication rates, though the claim that this doesn't affect conclusions could be more robust. There's a potential concern about effective sample size—while the paper reports statistics on N=1190, the exception_only condition has only ~607 unique inputs, meaning the model makes identical predictions on duplicates, which could inflate the apparent performance of the "2/1190 errors" metric.

More critically, this violates the statistical independence assumption underlying both Wilson confidence intervals and bootstrap tests. When duplicate inputs produce perfectly correlated predictions, the reported confidence intervals become artificially narrow and don't reflect true uncertainty. The paper should either report dedup-adjusted confidence intervals for exception_only or explicitly acknowledge that the reported intervals may be optimistic for high-duplication conditions.

Let me reconsider the actual impact on the numbers. If the 2 errors occur on unique inputs (IDs 901 and 905 with no duplicates themselves), then the unique accuracy would be 605/607 = 99.67%, not the reported 99.83% on 1190 samples. But I need to verify whether those error IDs themselves have duplicates in the test set. On unique inputs: 2/607 errors = 99.67% accuracy
- On all 1190: 2/1190 errors = 99.83% accuracy (duplicates of correct predictions inflate the denominator)

This means the reported 99.83% is somewhat inflated by duplicates. The "true" accuracy on unique inputs is 99.67%.

Actually, this depends on how duplicates are distributed. If the 583 duplicates (1190 - 607) are all copies of correct predictions, then removing them changes accuracy from 99.83% to (607-2)/607 = 99.67%. If some duplicates are copies of the 2 error samples, then they'd also be errors.

From the grep output, id=901 and id=905 are from HumanEval/95 `check_dict_case`, and they appear to be different solutions with the same function name. Looking at the test data, lines 902 and 906 both contain "check_dict_case" samples marked as pass with GeneratorExit errors, but other check_dict_case samples like lines 903 and 910 have different real errors and are correctly labeled as fail. So the question becomes whether the duplicate instances are clustered among the passing solutions, or if they're distributed across the dataset.

The concern is valid but not critical since the paper already discloses the dedup statistics, the 2/1190 error count is factually correct, and the statistical comparison still holds directionally even with an effective N of 607. The confidence interval would be slightly wider on the unique sample size but still strongly above other conditions, so the qualitative conclusions remain unchanged. I'll note this as a caveat but it doesn't alter the main findings.

The error analysis itself is thorough and accurate—the GeneratorExit explanation is correct and the distinction between model failure and representation ceiling is the right framing.

Now I'm checking whether the dedup rate is mentioned in the limitations section. The method section discloses it for transparency, but it's not called out as a limitation in the discussion. A reviewer could reasonably ask whether the confidence intervals are valid given 48.99% duplication. The paper should acknowledge that while duplicates don't affect point estimates or introduce leakage, they do reduce the effective degrees of freedom for statistical tests.

I'm also verifying the dedup numbers from the method section against my notes—the exception_only train, test, and raw_traces counts all match up correctly. The paper confirms zero train-test overlap by problem ID, which is good.

Overall, the work is solid with accurate numbers and appropriate disclosures, but the effective sample size issue for confidence intervals is the main thing that needs addressing before I write the review.
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Dv1jUhHsHFrFU6RRgmqGVx`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	
3	\subsection{Task Definition}
4	
5	We study \emph{verdict prediction}: given a Python function, a test input, and an optional execution trace, predict whether the test passes or fails.
6	The model produces a single token---\texttt{pass} or \texttt{fail}---making this a binary classification task.
7	Unlike code generation \citep{chen2025tracepile} or output prediction \citep{gu2024cruxeval}, verdict prediction isolates the model's ability to detect faults from its ability to simulate execution, providing a cleaner lens on what trace information actually contributes.
8	
9	\subsection{Dataset}
10	\label{sec:dataset}
11	
12	We sampled 10 candidate solutions per problem from Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct (temperature 0.8, top\_p 0.95) for 591 problems from HumanEval \citep{chen2021evaluating} (164 problems) and MBPP-sanitized \citep{austin2021program} (427 problems), executed each solution against canonical test cases with \texttt{sys.settrace} to obtain line-level execution traces and pass/fail verdicts, yielding 5,910 trace--verdict pairs (67\% pass, 33\% fail).
13	The dataset is split 80/20 by problem ID into 4,720 training and 1,190 test samples; the test set majority-class baseline is 71.51\%.
14	
15	\paragraph{Deduplication statistics.}
16	Because multiple passing solutions to the same problem produce identical (input, output) pairs in the \texttt{exception\_only} format---correct executions raise no exceptions, so the trace content is the same regardless of the solution's internal logic---the dataset contains duplicate rows.
17	Table~\ref{tab:dedup} reports the duplication rates across formats.
18	Train--test input overlap is zero (split by problem ID), so no information leakage occurs.
19	The high duplication in \texttt{exception\_only} does not affect our experimental conclusions: after deduplication the training set still contains 2,858 unique samples and the test set 607, providing sufficient coverage for the 591-problem benchmark.
20	For conditions with high input duplication (exception\_only), we report confidence intervals based on $N_\text{eff} = 607$ unique inputs to account for non-independence among repeated samples.
21	We report these statistics for transparency.
22	
23	\begin{table}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
27	\toprule
28	\textbf{Split} & \textbf{Total} & \textbf{Unique} & \textbf{Dup.\ rate} \\
29	\midrule
30	exception\_only train & 4{,}720 & 2{,}858 & 39.45\% \\
31	exception\_only test  & 1{,}190 &   607 & 48.99\% \\
32	raw\_traces (full)    & 5{,}910 & 5{,}803 &  1.81\% \\
33	\bottomrule
34	\end{tabular}
35	\caption{Deduplication statistics. The \texttt{exception\_only} format collapses multiple passing solutions into identical rows because correct executions produce no exception events. Train--test input overlap is 0 (no leakage).}
36	\label{tab:dedup}
37	\end{table}
38	
39	\subsection{Trace Representations}
40	
41	We define seven input conditions that systematically vary the trace information available to the model (Figure~\ref{fig:trace_overview}):
42	
43	\begin{itemize}
44	\item \texttt{no\_trace}: Source code and test input only; no execution information.
45	\item \texttt{full\_trace}: Complete line-by-line execution trace recording variable states at each step.
46	\item \texttt{VOTA} (Verdict-Oriented Trace Abstraction): Three compressed components---exception events, assertion-point comparisons, and loop compression summaries.
47	\item \texttt{exception\_only}: Only exception and error events extracted from the trace (e.g., \texttt{[ERROR] TypeError: ...}).
48	\item \texttt{ae\_only}: Only assertion-point actual/expected value pairs from the final comparison.
49	\item \texttt{loop\_only}: Only loop compression summaries (iteration counts and final variable states).
50	\item \texttt{VOTA\_no\_ae}: VOTA without assertion-point information (exception events + loop compression).
51	\end{itemize}
52	
53	These conditions span a spectrum from no trace information (\texttt{no\_trace}) through maximally compressed signals (\texttt{exception\_only}) to the complete unfiltered trace (\texttt{full\_trace}).
54	
55	\begin{figure*}[t]
56	\centering
57	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig1_trace_overview}
58	\caption{Overview of the verdict prediction pipeline. Source code and a test input are executed to produce a full raw trace. Four experimental conditions vary the trace information provided to the model: \texttt{no\_trace} (code only, 75.13\%), \texttt{full\_trace} (complete trace, 81.76\%), VOTA (compressed components, 99.41\%), and \texttt{exception\_only} (exception events only, 99.83\%). Each condition is paired with the same model to predict a pass/fail verdict. Accuracies shown are for Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B (seed=42, $N{=}1{,}190$).}
59	\label{fig:trace_overview}
60	\end{figure*}
61	
62	\subsection{Models}
63	
64	We evaluate two instruction-tuned code LLMs at the 7B parameter scale:
65	
66	\begin{itemize}
67	\item \textbf{Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct} \citep{qwen2025coder}: Pretrained on 5.5T tokens. Serves as the primary model for all ablation experiments.
68	\item \textbf{DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct} \citep{deepseek2024coder}: Pretrained on 2T tokens. Used for cross-model validation to test whether findings generalize beyond a single model family.
69	\end{itemize}
70	
71	The two models differ primarily in pretraining data volume (5.5T vs.\ 2T tokens), providing a natural axis for evaluating whether trace signal utility is model-dependent \citep{dong2024composition}.
72	
73	\subsection{Training Setup}
74	
75	All conditions use identical training configurations to ensure fair comparison.
76	We apply LoRA \citep{hu2022lora} (rank=16, $\alpha$=32, dropout=0.05) to all linear layers and train for 3 epochs with batch size 4, gradient accumulation 8 (effective batch size 32), learning rate $2 \times 10^{-4}$ with linear warmup and linear decay, warmup ratio 0.1, and weight decay 0.01.
77	The maximum sequence length is 2,048 tokens; input sequences exceeding this limit are truncated from the input content while preserving the complete verdict label.
78	Training wall-clock time ranges from 0.5 to 2.5 hours per condition on a single GPU, depending on trace format verbosity.
79	The primary seed is 42; seed robustness is evaluated with seeds 123 and 456 for the \texttt{exception\_only} condition.
80	
81	\subsection{Loss Ablation}
82	
83	To disentangle training signal quality from input signal quality, we introduce a \texttt{full\_trace\_label\_only} variant.
84	This condition uses the same full trace as input but computes loss only on the verdict token, masking all trace tokens during training (loss weight = 0 on trace positions).
85	If this variant improves over standard \texttt{full\_trace}, the improvement is attributable to \emph{gradient dilution}---trace tokens diluting the training signal without contributing to verdict prediction.
86	The remaining gap to VOTA is then attributable to \emph{information overload}---irrelevant trace content that the model must process at inference time.
```

</details>

      "content": "\\section{Method}\n\n\\subsection{Task Definition}\n\nWe study \\emph{verdict prediction}: given a Python function, a test input, and an optional execution trace, predict whether the test passes or fails.\nThe model produces a single token---\\texttt{pass} or \\texttt{fail}---making this a binary classification task.\nUnlike code generation \\citep{chen2025tracepile} or output prediction \\citep{gu2024cruxeval}, verdict prediction isolates the model's ability to detect faults from its ability to simulate execution, providing a cleaner lens on what trace information actually contributes.\n\n\\subsection{Dataset}\n\\label{sec:dataset}\n\nWe sampled 10 candidate solutions per problem from Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct (temperature 0.8, top\\_p 0.95) for 591 problems from HumanEval \\citep{chen2021evaluating} (164 problems) and MBPP-sanitized \\citep{austin2021program} (427 problems), executed each solution against canonical test cases with \\texttt{sys.settrace} to obtain line-level execution traces and pass/fail verdicts, yielding 5,910 trace--verdict pairs (67\\% pass, 33\\% fail).\nThe dataset is split 80/20 by problem ID into 4,720 training and 1,190 test samples; the test set majority-class baseline is 71.51\\%.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication statistics.}\nBecause multiple passing solutions to the same problem produce identical (input, output) pairs in the \\texttt{exception\\_only} format---correct executions raise no exceptions, so the trace content is the same regardless of the solution's internal logic---the dataset contains duplicate rows.\nTable~\\ref{tab:dedup} reports the duplication rates across formats.\nTrain--test input overlap is zero (split by problem ID), so no information leakage occurs.\nThe high duplication in \\texttt{exception\\_only} does not affect our experimental conclusions: after deduplication the training set still contains 2,858 unique samples and the test set 607, providing sufficient coverage for the 591-problem benchmark.\nFor conditions with high input duplication (exception\\_only), we report confidence intervals based on $N_\\text{eff} = 607$ unique inputs to account for non-independence among repeated samples.\nWe report these statistics for transparency.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Split} & \\textbf{Total} & \\textbf{Unique} & \\textbf{Dup.\\ rate} \\\\\n\\midrule\nexception\\_only train & 4{,}720 & 2{,}858 & 39.45\\% \\\\\nexception\\_only test  & 1{,}190 &   607 & 48.99\\% \\\\\nraw\\_traces (full)    & 5{,}910 & 5{,}803 &  1.81\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Deduplication statistics. The \\texttt{exception\\_only} format collapses multiple passing solutions into identical rows because correct executions produce no exception events. Train--test input overlap is 0 (no leakage).}\n\\label{tab:dedup}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Trace Representations}\n\nWe define seven input conditions that systematically vary the trace information available to the model (Figure~\\ref{fig:trace_overview}):\n\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\texttt{no\\_trace}: Source code and test input only; no execution information.\n\\item \\texttt{full\\_trace}: Complete line-by-line execution trace recording variable states at each step.\n\\item \\texttt{VOTA} (Verdict-Oriented Trace Abstraction): Three compressed components---exception events, assertion-point comparisons, and loop compression summaries.\n\\item \\texttt{exception\\_only}: Only exception and error events extracted from the trace (e.g., \\texttt{[ERROR] TypeError: ...}).\n\\item \\texttt{ae\\_only}: Only assertion-point actual/expected value pairs from the final comparison.\n\\item \\texttt{loop\\_only}: Only loop compression summaries (iteration counts and final variable states).\n\\item \\texttt{VOTA\\_no\\_ae}: VOTA without assertion-point information (exception events + loop compression).\n\\end{itemize}\n\nThese conditions span a spectrum from no trace information (\\texttt{no\\_trace}) through maximally compressed signals (\\texttt{exception\\_only}) to the complete unfiltered trace (\\texttt{full\\_trace}).\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig1_trace_overview}\n\\caption{Overview of the verdict prediction pipeline. Source code and a test input are executed to produce a full raw trace. Four experimental conditions vary the trace information provided to the model: \\texttt{no\\_trace} (code only, 75.13\\%), \\texttt{full\\_trace} (complete trace, 81.76\\%), VOTA (compressed components, 99.41\\%), and \\texttt{exception\\_only} (exception events only, 99.83\\%). Each condition is paired with the same model to predict a pass/fail verdict. Accuracies shown are for Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B (seed=42, $N{=}1{,}190$).}\n\\label{fig:trace_overview}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Models}\n\nWe evaluate two instruction-tuned code LLMs at the 7B parameter scale:\n\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\textbf{Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct} \\citep{qwen2025coder}: Pretrained on 5.5T tokens. Serves as the primary model for all ablation experiments.\n\\item \\textbf{DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct} \\citep{deepseek2024coder}: Pretrained on 2T tokens. Used for cross-model validation to test whether findings generalize beyond a single model family.\n\\end{itemize}\n\nThe two models differ primarily in pretraining data volume (5.5T vs.\\ 2T tokens), providing a natural axis for evaluating whether trace signal utility is model-dependent \\citep{dong2024composition}.\n\n\\subsection{Training Setup}\n\nAll conditions use identical training configurations to ensure fair comparison.\nWe apply LoRA \\citep{hu2022lora} (rank=16, $\\alpha$=32, dropout=0.05) to all linear layers and train for 3 epochs with batch size 4, gradient accumulation 8 (effective batch size 32), learning rate $2 \\times 10^{-4}$ with linear warmup and linear decay, warmup ratio 0.1, and weight decay 0.01.\nThe maximum sequence length is 2,048 tokens; input sequences exceeding this limit are truncated from the input content while preserving the complete verdict label.\nTraining wall-clock time ranges from 0.5 to 2.5 hours per condition on a single GPU, depending on trace format verbosity.\nThe primary seed is 42; seed robustness is evaluated with seeds 123 and 456 for the \\texttt{exception\\_only} condition.\n\n\\subsection{Loss Ablation}\n\nTo disentangle training signal quality from input signal quality, we introduce a \\texttt{full\\_trace\\_label\\_only} variant.\nThis condition uses the same full trace as input but computes loss only on the verdict token, masking all trace tokens during training (loss weight = 0 on trace positions).\nIf this variant improves over standard \\texttt{full\\_trace}, the improvement is attributable to \\emph{gradient dilution}---trace tokens diluting the training signal without contributing to verdict prediction.\nThe remaining gap to VOTA is then attributable to \\emph{information overload}---irrelevant trace content that the model must process at inference time.",
      "numLines": 86,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 86
    }
  },

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate our trace preprocessing pipeline across two research questions:
4	(1)~Do Tier~1 exception signals generalize across models of different capability?
5	(2)~What is the relative contribution of each trace component to verdict prediction accuracy?
6	All experiments use our trace--verdict dataset (\S\ref{sec:dataset}) with $N{=}1{,}190$ test samples; accuracy is reported with Wilson 95\% confidence intervals unless stated otherwise.
7	Pairwise comparisons use paired bootstrap resampling ($B{=}10{,}000$, seed=42); all cross-condition differences reported below are significant at $p{<}0.0001$ unless noted otherwise.
8	
9	\subsection{Cross-Model Comparison}
10	\label{sec:cross_model}
11	
12	To test whether exception-based trace compression transfers across model families, we compare Qwen-2.5-7B-Instruct and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct under four input conditions: \texttt{exception\_only} (Tier~1 exception events), VOTA (exception + assertion-point + loop compression), \texttt{full\_trace} (complete execution trace), and \texttt{no\_trace} (source code only).
13	
14	\begin{table}[t]
15	\centering
16	\small
17	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
18	\toprule
19	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Qwen-2.5-7B} & \textbf{DeepSeek-6.7B} & \textbf{Gap} \\
20	\midrule
21	exception\_only & \textbf{99.83}\% & 99.33\%$^\dag$ & 0.50pp \\
22	VOTA & 99.41\% & 84.45\% & 14.96pp \\
23	VOTA\_no\_ae & 99.41\%$^\S$ & --- & --- \\
24	full\_trace & 81.76\% & N/A$^\ddag$ & --- \\
25	no\_trace & 75.13\% & 73.36\% & 1.77pp \\
26	\midrule
27	Majority baseline & 71.51\% & 71.51\% & --- \\
28	\bottomrule
29	\end{tabular}
30	\caption{Cross-model accuracy ($N{=}1{,}190$). Qwen CIs: exception\_only [98.81, 99.91]$^*$, VOTA [98.82, 99.83], full\_trace [79.70, 83.90], no\_trace [72.77, 77.48]; DeepSeek CIs: VOTA [82.60, 86.40], no\_trace [70.80, 75.70]. $^\dag$DeepSeek exception\_only reached 99.83\% at epoch~1 but degraded to 99.33\% at convergence (single seed, no CI). $^\S$VOTA\_no\_ae evaluated on Qwen only; CI [98.90, 99.80]. $^\ddag$DeepSeek full\_trace was invalidated because the DeepSeek tokenizer produced sequences exceeding 2{,}048 tokens, causing the verdict label to be truncated during training; the model consequently failed to learn the classification task. $^*$CI computed with $N_\text{eff}{=}607$ unique inputs (\S\ref{sec:dataset}).}
31	\label{tab:cross_model}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	Table~\ref{tab:cross_model} reveals a striking asymmetry.
35	Under \texttt{exception\_only}, the cross-model gap is only 0.50 percentage points (pp), indicating that exception events carry a near-sufficient signal regardless of model capacity.
36	Under VOTA, the gap widens to 14.96pp: DeepSeek drops to 84.45\% while Qwen remains at 99.41\%.
37	This pattern suggests that processing mixed trace components (loop compressions, assertion points) demands model capability that the smaller DeepSeek lacks, whereas isolated exception events are interpretable by both models.
38	Per-class analysis (Appendix~\ref{sec:per_class}) reveals the mechanism: DeepSeek VOTA achieves 100\% fail precision but only 45.43\% fail recall, systematically defaulting to \texttt{pass}---in contrast to Qwen VOTA, which achieves 100\% fail recall.
39	
40	The \texttt{no\_trace} baseline confirms that source code alone provides limited signal (75.13\% for Qwen, 73.36\% for DeepSeek), only marginally above majority-class guessing (71.51\%).
41	
42	Seed robustness for Qwen \texttt{exception\_only} was evaluated across three seeds (42, 123, 456): all three produced identical accuracy of 99.83\% with exactly two errors each ($\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$).
43	
44	\subsection{Component Ablation}
45	\label{sec:ablation}
46	
47	We decompose the VOTA pipeline into individual components on Qwen-2.5-7B (seed=42).
48	Table~\ref{tab:ablation} arranges conditions into a two-tier hierarchy: Tier~1 signals (\texttt{ae\_only}, \texttt{exception\_only}) that individually achieve ${\geq}$99.8\% accuracy, and Tier~2 signals (\texttt{loop\_only}) that provide moderate improvement over the no-trace baseline.
49	
50	\begin{table}[t]
51	\centering
52	\small
53	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
54	\toprule
55	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Components} & \textbf{Acc (\%)} & \textbf{95\% CI} & \textbf{Tier} \\
56	\midrule
57	ae\_only & assertion-point & \textbf{99.92} & [99.75, 100.00] & 1 \\
58	exception\_only & exception events & 99.83 & [98.81, 99.91]$^*$ & 1 \\
59	VOTA & exc+ae+loop & 99.41 & [98.82, 99.83] & --- \\
60	VOTA\_no\_ae & exc+loop & 99.41 & [98.90, 99.80] & --- \\
61	loop\_only & loop compression & 85.63 & [83.53, 87.56] & 2 \\
62	full\_trace & complete trace & 81.76 & [79.70, 83.90] & --- \\
63	no\_trace & none & 75.13 & [72.77, 77.48] & --- \\
64	\bottomrule
65	\end{tabular}
66	\caption{Component ablation on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). Tier~1 signals individually achieve near-perfect accuracy; combining them (VOTA) slightly dilutes performance. $^*$CI computed with $N_\text{eff}{=}607$ unique inputs (\S\ref{sec:dataset}).}
67	\label{tab:ablation}
68	\end{table}
69	
70	Two findings stand out.
71	First, the Tier~1 signals are individually sufficient: \texttt{ae\_only} (99.92\%) and \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%) are not distinguishable ($p{=}0.80$, paired bootstrap, $B{=}10{,}000$), and both exceed VOTA (99.41\%, $p{=}0.01$ for \texttt{exception\_only} vs.\ VOTA, paired bootstrap).
72	This implies that combining signals in VOTA introduces minor noise rather than complementary information.
73	Second, removing assertion points from VOTA has no effect (VOTA\_no\_ae = VOTA = 99.41\%), confirming that within the VOTA mixture, exception events dominate the prediction signal.
74	
75	Tier~2 signal \texttt{loop\_only} (85.63\%) improves over \texttt{no\_trace} (75.13\%) by 10.50pp ($p{<}0.0001$, bootstrap 95\% CI $[{+}8.24, {+}12.86]$), showing that loop compression captures some execution semantics, but far less than exception events.
76	
77	\subsection{Information Density Decomposition}
78	\label{sec:density}
79	
80	The gap between \texttt{full\_trace} (81.76\%) and VOTA (99.41\%) is 17.65pp.
81	We decompose this gap into two mechanisms by introducing an intermediate condition, \texttt{full\_trace\_label\_only}, which retains the complete trace as input but computes loss only on the verdict token, masking all trace tokens during training.
82	
83	\begin{table}[t]
84	\centering
85	\small
86	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
87	\toprule
88	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Acc (\%)} & \textbf{$\Delta$ vs.\ full\_trace} & \textbf{Gap explained} \\
89	\midrule
90	full\_trace & 81.76 & --- & --- \\
91	label\_only & 86.22 & +4.46pp & 26\% \\
92	VOTA & 99.41 & +17.65pp & 100\% \\
93	\bottomrule
94	\end{tabular}
95	\caption{Information density decomposition. The full\_trace$\to$VOTA gap decomposes into gradient dilution (26\%, addressed by masking trace tokens from the loss) and information overload (74\%, addressed by removing irrelevant trace lines via VOTA compression).}
96	\label{tab:density}
97	\end{table}
98	
99	As shown in Table~\ref{tab:density}, the decomposition attributes 26\% of the gap (+4.46pp, $p{<}0.0001$, bootstrap 95\% CI $[{+}2.35, {+}6.55]$) to \emph{gradient dilution}---the training signal is diluted across trace tokens that do not contribute to verdict prediction---and 74\% (+13.19pp) to \emph{information overload}---the model must process trace lines irrelevant to the fault at inference time.
100	This two-factor decomposition is consistent across models: the VOTA cross-model gap (14.96pp, Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}) tracks model capability, suggesting that weaker models are disproportionately affected by information overload.
101	
102	\subsection{Calibration Analysis}
103	\label{sec:calibration}
104	
105	Beyond accuracy, we evaluate whether trace conditions affect prediction calibration, motivated by recent findings that code LLM confidence is often poorly calibrated \citep{spiess2025calibration}.
106	Figure~\ref{fig:calibration} shows reliability diagrams for four conditions on Qwen-2.5-7B.
107	
108	\begin{table}[t]
109	\centering
110	\small
111	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
112	\toprule
113	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{ECE $\downarrow$} & \textbf{Brier $\downarrow$} \\
114	\midrule
115	ae\_only & \textbf{0.0004} & \textbf{0.0008} \\
116	exception\_only & 0.003 & 0.0013 \\
117	VOTA & 0.005 & 0.005 \\
118	full\_trace & 0.096 & 0.144 \\
119	no\_trace & 0.171 & 0.197 \\
120	\bottomrule
121	\end{tabular}
122	\caption{Calibration metrics on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). ECE = expected calibration error; Brier = Brier score. Lower is better for both.}
123	\label{tab:calibration}
124	\end{table}
125	
126	Table~\ref{tab:calibration} shows that calibration quality mirrors the Tier~1/2 hierarchy.
127	Tier~1 conditions (\texttt{ae\_only}: ECE=0.0004; \texttt{exception\_only}: ECE=0.003) produce near-perfectly calibrated predictions, while \texttt{full\_trace} (ECE=0.096) and \texttt{no\_trace} (ECE=0.171) exhibit substantial miscalibration.
128	VOTA (ECE=0.005) is well-calibrated but slightly worse than either Tier~1 component alone, reinforcing the pattern that combining trace signals dilutes rather than improves the prediction signal.
129	
130	The Brier scores tell a consistent story: \texttt{ae\_only} (0.0008) and \texttt{exception\_only} (0.0013) achieve near-zero scores, while \texttt{no\_trace} (0.197) indicates substantial miscalibration.
131	
132	Figure~\ref{fig:cross_model_gap} visualizes the cross-model gap pattern, showing that exception-based conditions maintain tight cross-model agreement while VOTA and lower-tier conditions diverge.
133	
134	\begin{figure}[t]
135	\centering
136	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig2_calibration}
137	\caption{Reliability diagrams for four trace conditions on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). The Tier~1 condition (\texttt{exception\_only}) clusters near the diagonal (well-calibrated), while \texttt{full\_trace} and \texttt{no\_trace} exhibit systematic overconfidence.}
138	\label{fig:calibration}
139	\end{figure}
140	
141	\begin{figure}[t]
142	\centering
143	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig3_cross_model_gap}
144	\caption{Cross-model accuracy gap (Qwen $-$ DeepSeek) across trace conditions. Exception-based signals transfer across models (0.50pp gap), while VOTA is model-dependent (14.96pp gap).}
145	\label{fig:cross_model_gap}
146	\end{figure}

Tier~1 signals individually achieve near-perfect accuracy; combining them (VOTA) slightly dilutes performance. $^*$CI computed with $N_\\text{eff}{=}607$ unique inputs (\\S\\ref{sec:dataset}).}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\end{table}\n\nTwo findings stand out.\nFirst, the Tier~1 signals are individually sufficient: \\texttt{ae\\_only} (99.92\\%) and \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) are not distinguishable ($p{=}0.80$, paired bootstrap, $B{=}10{,}000$), and both exceed VOTA (99.41\\%, $p{=}0.01$ for \\texttt{exception\\_only} vs.\\ VOTA, paired bootstrap).\nThis implies that combining signals in VOTA introduces minor noise rather than complementary information.\nSecond, removing assertion points from VOTA has no effect (VOTA\\_no\\_ae = VOTA = 99.41\\%), confirming that within the VOTA mixture, exception events dominate the prediction signal.\n\nTier~2 signal \\texttt{loop\\_only} (85.63\\%) improves over \\texttt{no\\_trace} (75.13\\%) by 10.50pp ($p{<}0.0001$, bootstrap 95\\% CI $[{+}8.24, {+}12.86]$), showing that loop compression captures some execution semantics, but far less than exception events.\n\n\\subsection{Information Density Decomposition}\n\\label{sec:density}\n\nThe gap between \\texttt{full\\_trace} (81.76\\%) and VOTA (99.41\\%) is 17.65pp.\nWe decompose this gap into two mechanisms by introducing an intermediate condition, \\texttt{full\\_trace\\_label\\_only}, which retains the complete trace as input but computes loss only on the verdict token, masking all trace tokens during training.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Acc (\\%)} & \\textbf{$\\Delta$ vs.\\ full\\_trace} & \\textbf{Gap explained} \\\\\n\\midrule\nfull\\_trace & 81.76 & --- & --- \\\\\nlabel\\_only & 86.22 & +4.46pp & 26\\% \\\\\nVOTA & 99.41 & +17.65pp & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Information density decomposition. The full\\_trace$\\to$VOTA gap decomposes into gradient dilution (26\\%, addressed by masking trace tokens from the loss) and information overload (74\\%, addressed by removing irrelevant trace lines via VOTA compression).}\n\\label{tab:density}\n\\end{table}\n\nAs shown in Table~\\ref{tab:density}, the decomposition attributes 26\\% of the gap (+4.46pp, $p{<}0.0001$, bootstrap 95\\% CI $[{+}2.35, {+}6.55]$) to \\emph{gradient dilution}---the training signal is diluted across trace tokens that do not contribute to verdict prediction---and 74\\% (+13.19pp) to \\emph{information overload}---the model must process trace lines irrelevant to the fault at inference time.\nThis two-factor decomposition is consistent across models: the VOTA cross-model gap (14.96pp, Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}) tracks model capability, suggesting that weaker models are disproportionately affected by information overload.\n\n\\subsection{Calibration Analysis}\n\\label{sec:calibration}\n\nBeyond accuracy, we evaluate whether trace conditions affect prediction calibration, motivated by recent findings that code LLM confidence is often poorly calibrated \\citep{spiess2025calibration}.\nFigure~\\ref{fig:calibration} shows reliability diagrams for four conditions on Qwen-2.5-7B.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{ECE $\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{Brier $\\downarrow$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nae\\_only & \\textbf{0.0004} & \\textbf{0.0008} \\\\\nexception\\_only & 0.003 & 0.0013 \\\\\nVOTA & 0.005 & 0.005 \\\\\nfull\\_trace & 0.096 & 0.144 \\\\\nno\\_trace & 0.171 & 0.197 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Calibration metrics on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). ECE = expected calibration error; Brier = Brier score. Lower is better for both.}\n\\label{tab:calibration}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:calibration} shows that calibration quality mirrors the Tier~1/2 hierarchy.\nTier~1 conditions (\\texttt{ae\\_only}: ECE=0.0004; \\texttt{exception\\_only}: ECE=0.003) produce near-perfectly calibrated predictions, while \\texttt{full\\_trace} (ECE=0.096) and \\texttt{no\\_trace} (ECE=0.171) exhibit substantial miscalibration.\nVOTA (ECE=0.005) is well-calibrated but slightly worse than either Tier~1 component alone, reinforcing the pattern that combining trace signals dilutes rather than improves the prediction signal.\n\nThe Brier scores tell a consistent story: \\texttt{ae\\_only} (0.0008) and \\texttt{exception\\_only} (0.0013) achieve near-zero scores, while \\texttt{no\\_trace} (0.197) indicates substantial miscalibration.\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:cross_model_gap} visualizes the cross-model gap pattern, showing that exception-based conditions maintain tight cross-model agreement while VOTA and lower-tier conditions diverge.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig2_calibration}\n\\caption{Reliability diagrams for four trace conditions on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). The Tier~1 condition (\\texttt{exception\\_only}) clusters near the diagonal (well-calibrated), while \\texttt{full\\_trace} and \\texttt{no\\_trace} exhibit systematic overconfidence.}\n\\label{fig:calibration}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig3_cross_model_gap}\n\\caption{Cross-model accuracy gap (Qwen $-$ DeepSeek) across trace conditions. Exception-based signals transfer across models (0.50pp gap), while VOTA is model-dependent (14.96pp gap).}\n\\label{fig:cross_model_gap}\n\\end{figure}",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is an important finding. The paper reports accuracy = 99.83% (based on N=1190) but the CI [98.81, 99.91] is computed with p_hat = 605/607 = 99.67% and N=607. This creates an inconsistency: the point estimate and the CI are computed on different bases.

Specifically:
- Point estimate: 1188/1190 = 99.83% (on all samples)
- CI: Wilson CI for 605/607 = 99.67% with N=607 (on unique samples)


- The CI does technically contain 99.83%, but it's centered around 99.67%, which is a red flag for careful reviewers.

I need to decide how to resolve this: either report the accuracy on unique inputs with the corresponding CI, report both metrics on all samples with a recalculated CI, or stick with reporting accuracy on all samples but acknowledge the methodological choice.

The cleanest fix is probably option (a) — using N=1190 for both the accuracy and CI — since the train-test split by problem ID prevents information leakage anyway, making this statistically defensible.
```

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — CI/Neff 修复 (worker_paper_fix_ci_neff_20260515T195655)\n\n**结论**: WARNING\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: warning] **accuracy 与 CI 计算口径不一致**。Worker 的修改：\n  - Accuracy 仍报告 99.83%（基于 N=1190, 即 1188/1190）\n  - CI 改为 [98.81, 99.91]（基于 N_eff=607 unique inputs, 即 605/607 = 99.67%）\n  \n  **问题**：point estimate 99.83% 和 CI [98.81, 99.91] 是在不同的 N 上计算的。CI 的中心在 ~99.67%（unique accuracy），而 point estimate 是 99.83%（total accuracy）。这是统计不一致——严格审稿人会指出 \"your CI and point estimate are computed on different populations\"。\n\n  **验证**：用 Wilson CI 计算确认 [98.81, 99.91] 精确匹配 p̂=605/607, N=607，而非 p̂=1188/1190, N=607。\n\n- [severity: info] **修改范围正确**。Neff 标注仅应用于 exception_only（dedup 最严重的条件），其他条件未改动。method.tex 新增解释段落（L20），experiments.tex 表格添加 $^*$ 脚注。透明度好。\n\n- [severity: info] **不影响任何 claim 结论**。即使用更宽的 CI [98.81, 99.91]，exception_only 仍显著优于所有非 Tier-1 条件。但统计口径不一致本身是审稿人攻击面。\n\n### 建议\n\n三种修复方案（任选其一）：\n\n**(A) 回退到 N=1190 CI [99.58, 100.00]**（最简单）\n- 理由：模型对每个 test sample 独立推理，train-test 按 problem ID 划分无泄漏，N=1190 作为独立 Bernoulli trials 是合理的\n- 在 Limitations 中注明 \"exception_only test set contains 48.99% duplicate inputs; all CIs treat samples as independent\"\n\n**(B) 统一口径到 N_eff=607**\n- 报告 unique accuracy 99.67%（605/607）+ CI [98.81, 99.91]\n- 问题：改变 headline 数字，且 99.67% 低于 VOTA 99.41%... 等等，这不对。605/607=99.67% 仍高于 VOTA。但需要解释为什么 point estimate 变了。\n\n**(C) Cluster-robust bootstrap CI**（最严谨）\n- 每个 unique input 作为一个 cluster，在 cluster 层面做 bootstrap\n- 技术上最 defensible，但需要额外计算\n\n**推荐方案 A**——最简单，统计上 defensible，避免混乱。当前方案 (混合口径) 是最差选择。",

```text
15	\paragraph{Deduplication statistics.}
16	Because multiple passing solutions to the same problem produce identical (input, output) pairs in the \texttt{exception\_only} format---correct executions raise no exceptions, so the trace content is the same regardless of the solution's internal logic---the dataset contains duplicate rows.
17	Table~\ref{tab:dedup} reports the duplication rates across formats.
18	Train--test input overlap is zero (split by problem ID), so no information leakage occurs.
19	The high duplication in \texttt{exception\_only} does not affect our experimental conclusions: after deduplication the training set still contains 2,858 unique samples and the test set 607, providing sufficient coverage for the 591-problem benchmark.
20	The \texttt{exception\_only} test set contains 48.99\% duplicate inputs due to representation compactness; all confidence intervals treat samples as independent Bernoulli trials.
21	We report these statistics for transparency.
22	
```
